        Prior doc context:
Prior doc imported via --from-doc:
docs/design-thread-layer.md
Imported decisions:
- SD-001: Use the noun `thread` for a creative pursuit, not `workstream`/`session`/`pursuit`. The name appears in every `[thread]` prefix line and must match the creator's mental model
  rationale: chosen after explicit subagent debate; matches creator language ("pull on this thread"); short to type; the git overload is tolerable in the creative-tools domain.
  load_bearing: True
- SD-002: Tags-not-folders: every run writes a `runs/<slug>/run.json` with a `thread_id` field. The `runs/` directory stays flat. Re-grouping (split/merge/attach) is a metadata edit, never a `mv` operation
  rationale: coupling physical layout to logical grouping makes re-attribution destructive and breaks path references in older timelines. Identity is by ULID, never by path.
  load_bearing: True
- SD-003: A single index file `.artagents/threads.json` holds the thread map and `active_thread_id` pointer. All updates go through `fcntl.flock` on a sidecar lock file plus atomic write (tmp + fsync + `os.replace`), with a rotated `.bak`
  rationale: parallel runs must not lose updates; the index is small enough that single-file is correct.
  load_bearing: True
- SD-004: IDs are 26-char Crockford ULIDs (`run_id`, `thread_id`, `group_id`), monotonic within a process. References between records are always by ULID, never by path or label
  rationale: lex-sortable, stable across renames, no central registry needed.
  load_bearing: True
- SD-005: `parent_run_ids` on a run is **plural and causal**, derived from input-artifact hashes against producer `run_id`s — not the temporal previous run in the thread. A run with no consumed artifacts has empty `parent_run_ids` (it is a root)
  rationale: temporal parentage lies during interleaved work; causal parentage is reconstructible and honest.
  load_bearing: True
- SD-006: Auto-attribute decision function, evaluated at run start under the lock:
  rationale: lineage inference is the highest-ROI auto-attribution improvement; it removes a whole class of mis-grouping when the agent works across shells.
  load_bearing: False
- SD-007: Thread lifecycle: `open → archived → (re)open`. Archive triggers are explicit command, successful export/publish, or idle ≥ `IDLE_ARCHIVE_WINDOW` (default 7d). Reopen triggers are explicit command or new run within `IDLE_REOPEN_WINDOW`. Lazy enforcement on read; no daemon
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-008: `run.json` schema (frozen for v1 of the layer). Required fields:
  rationale: this is the irreversible part. Every run produced after sprint day 1 either has these fields or doesn't, forever. Metadata can be collected now and used by deferred features later.
  load_bearing: False
- SD-009: Brief snapshotting: every run writes `runs/<slug>/brief.copy.txt` and stamps `brief_content_sha256` in `run.json`. Briefs get edited in place between runs; without snapshotting, prompt diffs in the iteration video are unreconstructable
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-010: `external_service_calls` captures `model` and `model_version` per call, plus `seed`, `cost_usd`, `latency_ms`, and `request_id` where available. Models drift (gpt-image-1 → gpt-image-2 produces different outputs); `agent_version` (toolkit git sha) is insufficient on its own. `request_id` lets deleted external artifacts be re-fetched
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-011: `input_artifacts` records `produced_by_run_id` at the moment of consumption (computed from artifact sha256 against known runs). Without this, the causal DAG must be reconstructed later from hashes — possible but expensive and broken if intermediate runs are deleted
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-012: `host_id` = `sha256(hostname:user)[:16]`. Captured per run for provenance only. Not used for tracking, not transmitted. Lets a future sync tool reconcile cross-machine threads
  rationale: cheap to capture, expensive to add later if missed.
  load_bearing: False
- SD-013: Variants primitive: extend each `output_artifact` with `role` (`variant`/`ancillary`/`manifest`/`index`), `group` (= `sha256(run_id + group_label)[:16]`), `group_index`, and a free-form `variant_meta` blob. **Default role is `ancillary`. Executors must opt in to `variant`. Fail closed.**
  rationale: heterogeneous outputs (4 candidates + grid + manifest) cannot accidentally poison the variant group; executors that produce siblings declare them explicitly.
  load_bearing: True
- SD-014: Selection state lives at the thread, not the artifact. An append-only `.artagents/threads/<thread-id>/selections.jsonl` records each `{ts, group, kept[], discarded[], by, note?}` event. Never mutated. Current-keepers reduces over the log
  rationale: handles partial selection, deselection, and revision without losing history; concurrent picks are safe (last write wins on read).
  load_bearing: True
- SD-015: `chosen_from_groups: [{group, sha256}]` on a consuming run is a **separate edge** from `parent_run_ids`. The DAG distinguishes "descended from X" (causal) from "picked over its siblings" (curatorial). The iteration video needs both
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-016: Denormalized `.artagents/threads/<thread-id>/groups.json` index gives O(1) lookups for "what variants for run #N, which kept, which descended." Updated on run-write and selection-write
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-017: Agent gesture surface for variants — exactly five commands, one of which is mandatory-per-attempt:
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: False
- SD-018: Runner-emitted prefix lines on every `executors run` and `orchestrators run` invocation, written to stdout BEFORE the rest of the command output (so the agent sees them in tool result):
  rationale: the agent's working memory is the previous tool's stdout; thread state must be in that stream every turn.
  load_bearing: False
- SD-019: Provenance block in `hype.metadata.json` (added to `pipeline` block by `builtin.cut`). Carries `{thread_id, thread_label (denormalized), run_id, parent_run_ids[], chosen_from_groups[], contributing_runs[{run_id, thread_id, artifact_path, sha256}], starred, agent_version}`. Denormalization of `thread_label` and `agent_version` makes the artifact self-contained after `rm -rf .artagents/`
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-020: `builtin.iteration_video` is a single orchestrator polymorphic across modalities, not per-modality orchestrators. Real threads are heterogeneous (storyboard → animation, music video) and the agent shouldn't have to pick a modality before knowing what's in the thread
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-021: Iteration-video executor chain: `iteration.collect` → `iteration.summarize` (calls `builtin.understand` in parallel) → `iteration.score` → `iteration.assemble` → `builtin.cut` → `builtin.render`. Three new executors; the rest are reused. `iteration.assemble` is the only piece with non-trivial new visual logic
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-022: Iteration-video outputs (a single variant group):
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: False
- SD-023: Default ancestry is `provenance-graph` (walk `parent_run_ids` backward from target), not `thread`. Threads are a human construct that drift; causal parentage is hash-derived and doesn't lie. The HTML report labels which runs were "in-thread" vs "pulled in by ancestry."
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-024: Quality floor: `iteration.collect` computes a `data_quality` score in [0,1]. If below `quality_floor` (default 0.6), the orchestrator **refuses to render** and emits an actionable report listing the specific runs missing `parent_run_ids` with exact backfill commands. `--force` bypasses, logged into provenance
  rationale: silently rendering a video that misorders or omits half the runs destroys trust permanently. Refusal must be a helpful editor, not a bureaucrat.
  load_bearing: True
- SD-025: `ModalityRenderer` registry at `artagents/modalities/` (sibling to `elements/`, `executors/`, `orchestrators/`). Each renderer is a small declarative file declaring `kinds`, `clip_modes`, `default_clip_mode_for(shape, style)`, `produces_audio`, `cost_hint`. Discoverable via `python3 -m artagents modalities {list, inspect}`
  rationale: replaces a hardcoded global decision table that would become unmaintainable as modalities multiply. Each renderer owns its own scoped logic.
  load_bearing: True
- SD-026: Capability declarations on artifacts: extend `output_artifacts` with `preview_modes: [...]` and `duration` (where applicable). Producers declare what previews are tractable. The renderer registry resolves `(kind, preview_modes)` to a renderer at assembly time
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-027: Agent surface for iteration video — five flags + one inspect command total:
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: False
- SD-028: Audio-bed automatic selection: if `produces_audio` renderers cover >40% of clip duration → iterations-as-bed (cross-fade between audio variants). Else theme-declared bed. Else silence + subtle room tone. **Never generative music** — ages badly, fights the content
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-029: `generic_card` renderer is always registered as a fallback. When it fires, every iteration that uses it gets a "no renderer for `kind:<X>`" annotation in the HTML report and a stdout warning. Unknown modalities degrade gracefully and **loudly**
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-030: Single chokepoint integration: `run_executor()` at `artagents/executors/runner.py:75` and the orchestrator-runner equivalent are wrapped with `threads.begin(request)` / `threads.finalize(record_id, result)`. The wrapper is a no-op when: `dry_run=True`, `request.out` is unwritable or under `tempfile.gettempdir()`, `request.thread == "@none"`, or env `ARTAGENTS_THREADS_OFF=1` is set. **No executor's `run.py` is modified except where it produces variant outputs.**
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-031: Existing-executor patches required by this layer:
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: False
- SD-032: Hashing strategy: `xxhash` for `inputs_digest` (fast, non-cryptographic, used for dedup detection only). `sha256` for `output_artifacts[*].sha256` (one-shot at run end, never blocking the runner mid-run). Multi-GB renders must not block
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-033: In-flight reaper: any run start scans `runs/*/run.json` for records with `ended_at: null` whose owning process is gone (PID stamped at begin). Marks them `returncode: -1, ended_at: <now>, status: "orphaned"`. Lazy, runs at most once per process
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-034: Backwards compatibility is mandatory. Existing `runs/*/` directories without `run.json` continue to work unmodified. `python3 -m artagents thread backfill` scans, computes hashes, and adopts orphan dirs into auto-named threads (clustered by hash-graph connected components, not mtime). All existing tests must pass without modification
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-035: Sprint phasing constraints (load-bearing for the planner):
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: False
- SD-036: Sprint cuts (explicitly out of scope for the 2-week sprint, deferred to follow-up):
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: False
- SD-037: Test coverage required for week-1 ship:
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: False
- SD-038: Documentation deliverables for week-1 ship: `docs/threads.md` (one global doc explaining the model), one paragraph appended to `SKILL.md` instructing agents to run `thread show @active` at session start, footer line in `executors inspect` / `orchestrators inspect` output naming the active thread. **No per-tool `STAGE.md` is modified.**
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-039: Schema versioning: `run.json`, `threads.json`, `selections.jsonl`, `groups.json`, and the `provenance` block all carry `schema_version: int`. Readers accept current and N-1; writers always write current; one-time migration runs on first read at upgrade time, with `.bak`
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True
- SD-040: All paths in persisted JSON are repo-relative or content-addressed. No absolute paths. This makes thread export/sharing (a future feature) work for free as long as we hold the line now
  rationale: 
  load_bearing: True

        No prior clarification artifact exists. Identify ambiguities, ask clarifying questions, and state your assumptions inside the plan output.

        Requirements:
        - If the engineering brief suggests an approach, use it as your starting hypothesis — but before committing, consider if there's a simpler or more fundamental fix. The brief is well-researched input, not a final answer.
        - If the brief is absent, incomplete, or says "skip", inspect the repository yourself before planning.
        - Stay focused on the requested idea. If repo exploration surfaces unrelated issues or docs, ignore them and return to the task.
        - Prefer source code, tests, and directly relevant config files. Avoid `.megaplan/`, prior plan artifacts, and unrelated `docs/` or ops/deployment material unless the task explicitly depends on them.
        - Stop exploring once you have enough evidence to name the concrete touch points and validation path. Do not keep browsing after you can write the plan.
        - Produce a concrete implementation plan in markdown.
        - Define observable success criteria as objects with `criterion` (string) and `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`):
          - `must` — hard gate. The reviewer will block on failure. Use for correctness, functional requirements, and verifiable outcomes (e.g., "all existing tests pass", "API returns 200 for valid input"). Every `must` criterion must have a clear yes/no answer.
          - `should` — quality target. The reviewer flags but does not block. Use for subjective goals, numeric guidelines, and best-effort improvements (e.g., "file under ~300 lines", "no deeply nested conditionals", "each function has a single responsibility").
          - `info` — documented for humans, reviewer skips. Use for criteria that cannot be verified in this pipeline (e.g., "13 manual smoke tests pass", "stakeholder sign-off obtained").
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Use the `questions` field for ambiguities that would materially change implementation.
        - Use the `assumptions` field for defaults you are making so planning can proceed now.
        - Prefer cheap validation steps early.
        - Keep the plan proportional to the task. A 1-line fix needs a 2-step plan (apply fix + run tests), not a 5-step investigation.
        - If user notes answer earlier questions, incorporate them into the draft plan instead of re-asking them.
        - Fix the problem fully. Do not limit scope just to avoid breaking existing tests — update the tests too if needed.
        - Prefer the simplest, most direct fix. No fallbacks, type conversions, or defensive wrappers without concrete evidence they are needed.
        - If the task or issue hints suggest a specific approach, follow it. Only deviate with concrete counter-evidence.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

```
1	# Thread / Variant / Iteration-Video Layer — Design Context
2	
3	This document captures the design decisions converged on for adding a creative-iteration tracking layer to ArtAgents. It is the input to a sprint plan; it is not itself the sprint plan.
4	
5	The layer adds three things on top of the existing executor/orchestrator/elements model:
6	
7	1. **Threads** — automatic grouping of runs into creative pursuits.
8	2. **Variants** — sibling outputs of a single attempt, with selection semantics.
9	3. **Iteration video** — an orchestrator that renders a video walkthrough of any pursuit, polymorphic across modalities (image, audio, video, text, 3D, code).
10	
11	The whole system exists to support one killer demo: open a finished artifact, see the N runs that fed it (including sibling variants and prompt diffs) as a video.
12	
13	## Architectural premises
14	
15	- ArtAgents is **invoked by LLM agents** (Claude Code, etc.), not human operators. UX optimizes for an actor with no persistent memory across conversations.
16	- Every invocation produces files in `runs/<slug>/`. Today these are flat siblings; the new layer adds metadata stamping without changing the directory contract.
17	- The existing chokepoints are `run_executor()` in `artagents/executors/runner.py:75` and the orchestrator dispatch in `artagents/orchestrators/runner.py`. All new behavior lives in or above these wrappers — **no executor or orchestrator implementation is modified except where it produces variant outputs.**
18	- `.artagents/` is the canonical local-state root (already in `.gitignore`). New persistent state lives there.
19	- All path references in persisted JSON must be repo-relative or content-addressed. No absolute paths.
20	
21	## Settled Decisions
22	
23	- **SD-001** — Use the noun `thread` for a creative pursuit, not `workstream`/`session`/`pursuit`. The name appears in every `[thread]` prefix line and must match the creator's mental model. _load_bearing: true_
24	  Rationale: chosen after explicit subagent debate; matches creator language ("pull on this thread"); short to type; the git overload is tolerable in the creative-tools domain.
25	
26	- **SD-002** — Tags-not-folders: every run writes a `runs/<slug>/run.json` with a `thread_id` field. The `runs/` directory stays flat. Re-grouping (split/merge/attach) is a metadata edit, never a `mv` operation. _load_bearing: true_
27	  Rationale: coupling physical layout to logical grouping makes re-attribution destructive and breaks path references in older timelines. Identity is by ULID, never by path.
28	
29	- **SD-003** — A single index file `.artagents/threads.json` holds the thread map and `active_thread_id` pointer. All updates go through `fcntl.flock` on a sidecar lock file plus atomic write (tmp + fsync + `os.replace`), with a rotated `.bak`. _load_bearing: true_
30	  Rationale: parallel runs must not lose updates; the index is small enough that single-file is correct.
31	
32	- **SD-004** — IDs are 26-char Crockford ULIDs (`run_id`, `thread_id`, `group_id`), monotonic within a process. References between records are always by ULID, never by path or label. _load_bearing: true_
33	  Rationale: lex-sortable, stable across renames, no central registry needed.
34	
35	- **SD-005** — `parent_run_ids` on a run is **plural and causal**, derived from input-artifact hashes against producer `run_id`s — not the temporal previous run in the thread. A run with no consumed artifacts has empty `parent_run_ids` (it is a root). _load_bearing: true_
36	  Rationale: temporal parentage lies during interleaved work; causal parentage is reconstructible and honest.
37	
38	- **SD-006** — Auto-attribute decision function, evaluated at run start under the lock:
39	  1. Explicit `--thread <id>` or `@new` / `@none` wins.
40	  2. **Lineage inference**: if any input arg points inside `runs/<R>/`, inherit `R`'s thread.
41	  3. Else active pointer is `open` → join.
42	  4. Else active is `archived` and within `IDLE_REOPEN_WINDOW` (default 48h) → reopen and join.
43	  5. Else create new thread.
44	  _load_bearing: true_
45	  Rationale: lineage inference is the highest-ROI auto-attribution improvement; it removes a whole class of mis-grouping when the agent works across shells.
46	
47	- **SD-007** — Thread lifecycle: `open → archived → (re)open`. Archive triggers are explicit command, successful export/publish, or idle ≥ `IDLE_ARCHIVE_WINDOW` (default 7d). Reopen triggers are explicit command or new run within `IDLE_REOPEN_WINDOW`. Lazy enforcement on read; no daemon. _load_bearing: true_
48	
49	- **SD-008** — `run.json` schema (frozen for v1 of the layer). Required fields:
50	  ```
51	  schema_version, run_id, thread_id, parent_run_ids[], chosen_from_groups[],
52	  executor_id, orchestrator_id, kind, started_at, ended_at, returncode,
53	  out_path, cli_args_redacted, agent_version, host_id,
54	  brief_content_sha256, inputs_digest,
55	  input_artifacts: [{path, sha256, produced_by_run_id, kind}],
56	  output_artifacts: [{path, sha256, kind, role, group, group_index, preview_modes, duration, variant_meta}],
57	  external_service_calls: [{service, request_id, model, model_version, seed?, cost_usd?, latency_ms}],
58	  starred: bool
59	  ```
60	  _load_bearing: true_
61	  Rationale: this is the irreversible part. Every run produced after sprint day 1 either has these fields or doesn't, forever. Metadata can be collected now and used by deferred features later.
62	
63	- **SD-009** — Brief snapshotting: every run writes `runs/<slug>/brief.copy.txt` and stamps `brief_content_sha256` in `run.json`. Briefs get edited in place between runs; without snapshotting, prompt diffs in the iteration video are unreconstructable. _load_bearing: true_
64	
65	- **SD-010** — `external_service_calls` captures `model` and `model_version` per call, plus `seed`, `cost_usd`, `latency_ms`, and `request_id` where available. Models drift (gpt-image-1 → gpt-image-2 produces different outputs); `agent_version` (toolkit git sha) is insufficient on its own. `request_id` lets deleted external artifacts be re-fetched. _load_bearing: true_
66	
67	- **SD-011** — `input_artifacts` records `produced_by_run_id` at the moment of consumption (computed from artifact sha256 against known runs). Without this, the causal DAG must be reconstructed later from hashes — possible but expensive and broken if intermediate runs are deleted. _load_bearing: true_
68	
69	- **SD-012** — `host_id` = `sha256(hostname:user)[:16]`. Captured per run for provenance only. Not used for tracking, not transmitted. Lets a future sync tool reconcile cross-machine threads. _load_bearing: false_
70	  Rationale: cheap to capture, expensive to add later if missed.
71	
72	- **SD-013** — Variants primitive: extend each `output_artifact` with `role` (`variant`/`ancillary`/`manifest`/`index`), `group` (= `sha256(run_id + group_label)[:16]`), `group_index`, and a free-form `variant_meta` blob. **Default role is `ancillary`. Executors must opt in to `variant`. Fail closed.** _load_bearing: true_
73	  Rationale: heterogeneous outputs (4 candidates + grid + manifest) cannot accidentally poison the variant group; executors that produce siblings declare them explicitly.
74	
75	- **SD-014** — Selection state lives at the thread, not the artifact. An append-only `.artagents/threads/<thread-id>/selections.jsonl` records each `{ts, group, kept[], discarded[], by, note?}` event. Never mutated. Current-keepers reduces over the log. _load_bearing: true_
76	  Rationale: handles partial selection, deselection, and revision without losing history; concurrent picks are safe (last write wins on read).
77	
78	- **SD-015** — `chosen_from_groups: [{group, sha256}]` on a consuming run is a **separate edge** from `parent_run_ids`. The DAG distinguishes "descended from X" (causal) from "picked over its siblings" (curatorial). The iteration video needs both. _load_bearing: true_
79	
80	- **SD-016** — Denormalized `.artagents/threads/<thread-id>/groups.json` index gives O(1) lookups for "what variants for run #N, which kept, which descended." Updated on run-write and selection-write. _load_bearing: true_
81	
82	- **SD-017** — Agent gesture surface for variants — exactly five commands, one of which is mandatory-per-attempt:
83	  ```
84	  thread keep <run-id>:<n>[,<n>]      # mandatory when variants exist
85	  thread keep <run-id>:none           # explicit dismiss (silences the [variants] nag)
86	  thread group <run-id> <run-id> ...  # post-hoc retroactive grouping
87	  --variants N                        # request a sibling group up front
88	  --from <run-id>:<n>                 # consume a specific variant; defaults to most-recent kept in thread
89	  ```
90	  No more, no less. _load_bearing: true_
91	
92	- **SD-018** — Runner-emitted prefix lines on every `executors run` and `orchestrators run` invocation, written to stdout BEFORE the rest of the command output (so the agent sees them in tool result):
93	  ```
94	  [thread]   <label> · run #N · parent #M[:vK]
95	  [variants] run #N produced K siblings — none kept (use: thread keep N:K)   # only when unresolved variants exist
96	  ```
97	  Plus tiered Notice / Warn lines for first-run-of-process, gap >1h, brief novelty, auto-reopen of archived thread. _load_bearing: true_
98	  Rationale: the agent's working memory is the previous tool's stdout; thread state must be in that stream every turn.
99	
100	- **SD-019** — Provenance block in `hype.metadata.json` (added to `pipeline` block by `builtin.cut`). Carries `{thread_id, thread_label (denormalized), run_id, parent_run_ids[], chosen_from_groups[], contributing_runs[{run_id, thread_id, artifact_path, sha256}], starred, agent_version}`. Denormalization of `thread_label` and `agent_version` makes the artifact self-contained after `rm -rf .artagents/`. _load_bearing: true_
101	
102	- **SD-020** — `builtin.iteration_video` is a single orchestrator polymorphic across modalities, not per-modality orchestrators. Real threads are heterogeneous (storyboard → animation, music video) and the agent shouldn't have to pick a modality before knowing what's in the thread. _load_bearing: true_
103	
104	- **SD-021** — Iteration-video executor chain: `iteration.collect` → `iteration.summarize` (calls `builtin.understand` in parallel) → `iteration.score` → `iteration.assemble` → `builtin.cut` → `builtin.render`. Three new executors; the rest are reused. `iteration.assemble` is the only piece with non-trivial new visual logic. _load_bearing: true_
105	
106	- **SD-022** — Iteration-video outputs (a single variant group):
107	  ```
108	  iteration.mp4              # the rendered video (chosen variant)
109	  iteration.timeline.json    # editable, re-renderable via builtin.render
110	  iteration.manifest.json    # ordered run list with allocations
111	  iteration.report.html      # ancillary; the real artifact for timeline-heavy threads
112	  iteration.quality.json     # missing/inferred per iteration
113	  ```
114	  _load_bearing: true_
115	
116	- **SD-023** — Default ancestry is `provenance-graph` (walk `parent_run_ids` backward from target), not `thread`. Threads are a human construct that drift; causal parentage is hash-derived and doesn't lie. The HTML report labels which runs were "in-thread" vs "pulled in by ancestry." _load_bearing: true_
117	
118	- **SD-024** — Quality floor: `iteration.collect` computes a `data_quality` score in [0,1]. If below `quality_floor` (default 0.6), the orchestrator **refuses to render** and emits an actionable report listing the specific runs missing `parent_run_ids` with exact backfill commands. `--force` bypasses, logged into provenance. _load_bearing: true_
119	  Rationale: silently rendering a video that misorders or omits half the runs destroys trust permanently. Refusal must be a helpful editor, not a bureaucrat.
120	
121	- **SD-025** — `ModalityRenderer` registry at `artagents/modalities/` (sibling to `elements/`, `executors/`, `orchestrators/`). Each renderer is a small declarative file declaring `kinds`, `clip_modes`, `default_clip_mode_for(shape, style)`, `produces_audio`, `cost_hint`. Discoverable via `python3 -m artagents modalities {list, inspect}`. _load_bearing: true_
122	  Rationale: replaces a hardcoded global decision table that would become unmaintainable as modalities multiply. Each renderer owns its own scoped logic.
123	
124	- **SD-026** — Capability declarations on artifacts: extend `output_artifacts` with `preview_modes: [...]` and `duration` (where applicable). Producers declare what previews are tractable. The renderer registry resolves `(kind, preview_modes)` to a renderer at assembly time. _load_bearing: true_
125	
126	- **SD-027** — Agent surface for iteration video — five flags + one inspect command total:
127	  ```
128	  iteration_video inspect <thread>          # primary discovery; prints detected modalities, chosen renderers, defaults, --why reasoning
129	  --renderers image=grid,audio=waveform,video=pip
130	  --clip-mode <run-id>=pip                  # single-iteration override (escape hatch)
131	  --direction "natural language"            # one-off creative direction (parsed into structured hints; falls back gracefully)
132	  --mode chaptered|parallel|interleaved     # for cross-modal threads (default: chaptered)
133	  --audio-bed iterations|theme|silence|<path>
134	  ```
135	  Precedence: theme > direction > style preset > defaults. _load_bearing: true_
136	
137	- **SD-028** — Audio-bed automatic selection: if `produces_audio` renderers cover >40% of clip duration → iterations-as-bed (cross-fade between audio variants). Else theme-declared bed. Else silence + subtle room tone. **Never generative music** — ages badly, fights the content. _load_bearing: true_
138	
139	- **SD-029** — `generic_card` renderer is always registered as a fallback. When it fires, every iteration that uses it gets a "no renderer for `kind:<X>`" annotation in the HTML report and a stdout warning. Unknown modalities degrade gracefully and **loudly**. _load_bearing: true_
140	
141	- **SD-030** — Single chokepoint integration: `run_executor()` at `artagents/executors/runner.py:75` and the orchestrator-runner equivalent are wrapped with `threads.begin(request)` / `threads.finalize(record_id, result)`. The wrapper is a no-op when: `dry_run=True`, `request.out` is unwritable or under `tempfile.gettempdir()`, `request.thread == "@none"`, or env `ARTAGENTS_THREADS_OFF=1` is set. **No executor's `run.py` is modified except where it produces variant outputs.** _load_bearing: true_
142	
143	- **SD-031** — Existing-executor patches required by this layer:
144	  - `artagents/executors/generate_image/run.py` — declare `role: variant` on the N images; emit `group` from `(run_id, prompt_index)`; populate `preview_modes` and `duration`.
145	  - `artagents/orchestrators/logo_ideas/run.py` — fold existing rich per-candidate metadata (`name`, `rationale`, `prompt`, `generated.*`) into `variant_meta` and stamp `role: variant`, `group`, `group_index`.
146	  - All other executors are unmodified for v1.
147	  _load_bearing: true_
148	
149	- **SD-032** — Hashing strategy: `xxhash` for `inputs_digest` (fast, non-cryptographic, used for dedup detection only). `sha256` for `output_artifacts[*].sha256` (one-shot at run end, never blocking the runner mid-run). Multi-GB renders must not block. _load_bearing: true_
150	
151	- **SD-033** — In-flight reaper: any run start scans `runs/*/run.json` for records with `ended_at: null` whose owning process is gone (PID stamped at begin). Marks them `returncode: -1, ended_at: <now>, status: "orphaned"`. Lazy, runs at most once per process. _load_bearing: true_
152	
153	- **SD-034** — Backwards compatibility is mandatory. Existing `runs/*/` directories without `run.json` continue to work unmodified. `python3 -m artagents thread backfill` scans, computes hashes, and adopts orphan dirs into auto-named threads (clustered by hash-graph connected components, not mtime). All existing tests must pass without modification. _load_bearing: true_
154	
155	- **SD-035** — Sprint phasing constraints (load-bearing for the planner):
156	  - **Week 1 (days 1–5) is the data layer.** Thread schema, locking, atomic write, `run_executor` wrapper, auto-attribute, prefix lines, `thread {new, list, show, archive, reopen}` CLI, variants (`role`/`group`/`selections.jsonl`/`groups.json`/`chosen_from_groups`), `[variants]` nag, `thread keep`, executor patches, brief snapshotting, in-flight reaper, provenance block in `hype.metadata.json`, `preview_modes`/`duration` on artifacts. **All metadata fields in SD-008 must ship by end of week 1**, because every run after sprint day 1 either has them or doesn't, forever.
157	  - **Week 2 (days 6–10) is the iteration video MVP.** `iteration.collect`, `iteration.summarize`, `iteration.score`, `iteration.assemble`, `builtin.iteration_video`, HTML report, quality floor, modality registry framework with **3 renderers only** (`image_grid`, `audio_waveform`, `generic_card`), `inspect` subcommand, end-to-end demo render against an existing thread (`runs/artagents_logo_v3` is a known-good fixture).
158	  _load_bearing: true_
159	
160	- **SD-036** — Sprint cuts (explicitly out of scope for the 2-week sprint, deferred to follow-up):
161	  - `thread {split, merge, attach, detach, gc}` (only `backfill` ships in v1)
162	  - 4 of 7 modality renderers: `video_pip`, `text_diff`, `model_turntable`, `code_scroll`
163	  - Cross-modal sub-pursuits and `--mode parallel|interleaved`
164	  - `--direction` natural-language parsing (flag accepted but treated as label only)
165	  - `--why` reasoning surface on `iteration_video inspect`
166	  - `thread doctor` auto-fix suggestions (detection-only smell line ships in v1)
167	  - `cut --variants N` (timeline variants)
168	  - Brief-similarity heuristics, semantic-distance dilation
169	  - Human-facing browse UI for threads
170	  _load_bearing: true_
171	
172	- **SD-037** — Test coverage required for week-1 ship:
173	  - Schema round-trip + version migration N-1 → N
174	  - Atomic write + lock acquisition + partial-write recovery via `.bak`
175	  - Auto-attribute decision function (every branch, including lineage inheritance) with frozen clock
176	  - Concurrency: 8 parallel subprocess runs, all recorded, no lost updates
177	  - `run_executor` integration end-to-end: `run.json` written, index updated, finalization records returncode
178	  - Orchestrator integration: nested executor inherits parent thread via env
179	  - Backwards compat: `ARTAGENTS_THREADS_OFF=1` produces no artifacts and no errors; missing `.artagents/` is auto-created; existing `runs/*/` without `run.json` work unchanged
180	  - Variants: heterogeneous outputs (variant + ancillary + manifest) result in only the variants being grouped
181	  - Provenance: `hype.metadata.json` carries the block; `thread_label` survives index deletion
182	  - All existing pytest tests pass without modification
183	  _load_bearing: true_
184	
185	- **SD-038** — Documentation deliverables for week-1 ship: `docs/threads.md` (one global doc explaining the model), one paragraph appended to `SKILL.md` instructing agents to run `thread show @active` at session start, footer line in `executors inspect` / `orchestrators inspect` output naming the active thread. **No per-tool `STAGE.md` is modified.** _load_bearing: true_
186	
187	- **SD-039** — Schema versioning: `run.json`, `threads.json`, `selections.jsonl`, `groups.json`, and the `provenance` block all carry `schema_version: int`. Readers accept current and N-1; writers always write current; one-time migration runs on first read at upgrade time, with `.bak`. _load_bearing: true_
188	
189	- **SD-040** — All paths in persisted JSON are repo-relative or content-addressed. No absolute paths. This makes thread export/sharing (a future feature) work for free as long as we hold the line now. _load_bearing: true_
190	
191	## Open questions (for the sprint plan to resolve)
192	
193	These are not settled; the sprint plan should make a call:
194	
195	- Day-by-day allocation within each week (the broad week split is settled; the per-day breakdown is not).
196	- Whether `iteration.summarize` parallelism uses Python threads, asyncio, or sequential calls in the v1 MVP.
197	- Exact format of the prefix line beyond what SD-018 specifies (terminal width handling, color, truncation rules).
198	- Whether the in-flight reaper (SD-033) runs at process start or lazily on first index read.
199	- Where the `WORKSTREAMS.md` / `THREADS.md` global doc lives (likely `docs/threads.md`, but confirm against repo conventions).
200	- Concrete `quality_floor` formula in `iteration.collect`: which signals contribute and with what weights.
201	
202	## Non-goals
203	
204	- Cross-machine sync of threads. (Capture `host_id` per SD-012 to enable later.)
205	- Public API stability of any of the new schemas across major versions.
206	- Migration tooling for runs produced before the layer ships. (Backfill per SD-034 is best-effort.)
207	- A general-purpose web UI for browsing threads. (`iteration.report.html` per orchestrator output is the only HTML deliverable.)
208

28	def _pipeline_module():

35	    pipeline = _pipeline_module()
36	    steps = {step.name: step for step in pipeline.build_pool_steps()}
37	    missing = [name for name in pipeline.STEP_ORDER if name not in steps]
38	    if missing:

40	    return MappingProxyType(steps)

75	def run_executor(request: ExecutorRunRequest, registry: ExecutorRegistry | None = None) -> ExecutorRunResult:
76	    active_registry = registry or load_default_registry()
77	    executor = active_registry.get(request.executor_id)
78	    if executor.id == "upload.youtube":
79	        return _run_upload_youtube(request)
80	    values = _request_values(request)
81	    _validate_required_inputs(executor, values)
82	    condition_result = evaluate_conditions(executor, values)
83	    if condition_result.skipped:
84	        return ExecutorRunResult(
85	            executor_id=executor.id,
86	            kind=executor.kind,
87	            payload={"executor_id": executor.id, "skipped": True, "skipped_reason": condition_result.reason},
88	            dry_run=request.dry_run,
89	            skipped=True,
90	            skipped_reason=condition_result.reason,
91	        )
92	
93	    missing_binaries = check_executor_binaries(executor) if request.check_binaries else ()
94	    if missing_binaries:
95	        return ExecutorRunResult(
96	            executor_id=executor.id,
97	            kind=executor.kind,
98	            payload={"executor_id": executor.id, "missing_binaries": list(missing_binaries)},
99	            dry_run=request.dry_run,
100	            missing_binaries=missing_binaries,
101	        )
102	
103	    if executor.kind == "built_in" and "pipeline_step" in executor.metadata:
104	        return _run_builtin_executor(executor, request)
105	    return _run_external_executor(executor, request, values)
106	
107	
108	def _run_upload_youtube(request: ExecutorRunRequest) -> ExecutorRunResult:
109	    inputs = dict(request.inputs)
110	    if request.dry_run:
111	        return ExecutorRunResult(
112	            executor_id=request.executor_id,
113	            kind="built_in",
114	            dry_run=True,
115	            payload={"would_run": "upload.youtube", "inputs": inputs},
116	        )
117	
118	    from artagents.executors.upload_youtube.src.social_publish import publish_youtube_video
119	
120	    result = publish_youtube_video(
121	        video_url=_required_input(inputs, "video_url"),
122	        title=_required_input(inputs, "title"),
123	        description=_required_input(inputs, "description"),
124	        tags=_optional_input(inputs, "tags") or _optional_input(inputs, "tag"),
125	        privacy_status=str(_optional_input(inputs, "privacy_status") or "private"),
126	        playlist_id=_optional_input(inputs, "playlist_id"),
127	        made_for_kids=bool(_optional_input(inputs, "made_for_kids") or False),
128	    )
129	    return ExecutorRunResult(executor_id=request.executor_id, kind="built_in", payload=result)
130
```

# Implementation Plan: Sprint Plan Document for Thread/Variant/Iteration-Video Layer

## Overview
Produce `docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`: a 2-week (10 working day) sprint plan that turns `docs/design-thread-layer.md`'s Settled Decisions into day-by-day deliverables. The deliverable is a **planning document**, not code. Every Settled Decision (SD-001…SD-040) is a hard constraint; SD-035 fixes the week split, SD-036 fixes scope cuts. The document must include: one-page exec summary, day-by-day plan with per-day acceptance criteria, critical path, risks + mitigations, integration test plan (rolls up SD-037), dogfood plan against `runs/artagents_logo_v3`, and explicit resolutions to the six Open Questions in the design doc.

Repo grounding (already verified):
- Chokepoint exists: `artagents/executors/runner.py:75` (`run_executor`), and `artagents/orchestrators/runner.py`.
- Variant-producing tools exist: `artagents/executors/generate_image/run.py`, `artagents/orchestrators/logo_ideas/run.py`.
- Fixture exists: `runs/artagents_logo_v3/` with `concepts.json`, `grid.jpg`, `logo-manifest.json`, `logo-plan.json`, `prompts.json`, `images/logo-00N.png`.
- `tests/` is pytest-based with substantial existing coverage that must remain green (SD-034, SD-037).
- `SKILL.md` exists at repo root (touchpoint for SD-038).
- `.artagents/` is canonical local-state root (already in `.gitignore` per design doc).

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Author the exec summary (`docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Open** the document with `# Sprint Plan: Thread / Variant / Iteration-Video Layer`.
2. **Write** a one-page `## Executive Summary` (target ~40 lines) covering:
   - Goal: ship the data layer (Week 1) + iteration-video MVP (Week 2) so every run after Day 1 has SD-008 metadata forever.
   - Why now: SD-008 is the irreversible part — runs without it are unrecoverable; lineage drift is silently destructive.
   - Scope in: SD-035 (data layer + 3 renderers + 3 new executors).
   - Scope out: SD-036 verbatim (split/merge/attach/detach/gc, 4 of 7 renderers, parallel/interleaved modes, `--direction` parsing, `--why`, `thread doctor` autofix, `cut --variants`, brief-similarity, browse UI).
   - Critical path: Day 1 lock+atomic-write → Day 2 schema+wrapper → Day 3 auto-attribute+prefix → Day 4 variants → Day 5 brief+provenance → Days 6–10 iteration video on top.
   - Top 3 risks (chokepoint regression, lock starvation under 8-way parallelism, fixture insufficiency) each with one-line mitigations.
   - Definition of done: dogfood render of `runs/artagents_logo_v3` produces `iteration.mp4` + `iteration.report.html` + `iteration.timeline.json` + `iteration.manifest.json` + `iteration.quality.json`; full pytest suite passes; SD-037 integration tests added and green.

### Step 2: Author the constraints recap (`docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `## Hard Constraints (from design)` listing the load-bearing SDs as a checklist mapped to where they land in the plan, e.g. `SD-003 atomic-write index → Day 1`, `SD-008 schema frozen → Day 2 (must ship by EoW1)`, `SD-013 variant role → Day 4`, `SD-019 hype.metadata.json provenance → Day 5`, `SD-024 quality floor → Day 8`, `SD-029 generic_card loud fallback → Day 9`. Group by week.
2. **Add** a one-paragraph "fail-closed" reminder: default `role` is `ancillary`; only `generate_image` and `logo_ideas` opt in to `variant` in v1 (SD-031). No other executor's `run.py` is modified.

### Step 3: Author resolutions to Open Questions (`docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `## Open Question Resolutions`. One subsection per OQ in the design doc, each stating decision + rationale + which day it lands in:
   - **OQ-1 Day-by-day allocation** → see `## Week 1` and `## Week 2` below (this whole document is the resolution).
   - **OQ-2 `iteration.summarize` parallelism** → **resolved: Python `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor`, max_workers=4**. Rationale: `builtin.understand` is I/O-bound (subprocess + network); threads avoid the asyncio rewrite and are easy to reason about under v1 quality floor; matches existing patterns in repo.
   - **OQ-3 Prefix line format** → **resolved**: width-bounded to `min(120, $COLUMNS)`; ANSI color only when `sys.stdout.isatty()` and `$NO_COLOR` is unset; truncate `<label>` to 32 chars with `…`; never truncate run/parent IDs. Notice/Warn lines emitted on separate lines.
   - **OQ-4 Reaper timing (SD-033)** → **resolved: lazy on first index read per process**, gated by a process-local "ran-this-process" flag. Rationale: SD-033 says lazy; running at process start adds startup latency on every CLI invocation.
   - **OQ-5 Global doc location** → **resolved: `docs/threads.md`** (matches repo convention — `docs/` already holds onboarding/design docs, and SD-038 names this exact path).
   - **OQ-6 `quality_floor` formula** → **resolved**: `data_quality = 0.5 * fraction_with_parent_run_ids + 0.3 * fraction_with_brief_content_sha256 + 0.2 * fraction_with_at_least_one_input_artifact_resolved`. Default floor 0.6 per SD-024. Each missing-signal contributes a specific backfill suggestion in the refusal report.

### Step 4: Author Week 1 — Data Layer days 1–5 (`docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Add** `## Week 1: Data Layer (Days 1–5)` with one `### Day N` subsection per day. For each day write: **Goal**, **Deliverables (file paths)**, **SDs landed**, **Acceptance criteria** (each yes/no checkable), **Tests added**. Map content as follows:

   - **Day 1 — Index foundation + IDs.** Goal: durable index. Deliverables: `artagents/threads/__init__.py`, `artagents/threads/ids.py` (Crockford ULID, monotonic-within-process per SD-004), `artagents/threads/index.py` (load/save `.artagents/threads.json` with `fcntl.flock` sidecar, atomic write via tmp + fsync + `os.replace`, rotated `.bak` per SD-003), `artagents/threads/schema.py` (typed dicts, `schema_version: 1` on every record per SD-039). Acceptance: 8 parallel writes preserve all updates; corrupted file recovers from `.bak`; all paths repo-relative (SD-040). Tests: schema round-trip; lock contention (8 subprocesses); partial-write recovery.

Deliverables: `artagents/threads/record.py` (write `runs/<slug>/run.json` with all SD-008 fields including empty-but-present `parent_run_ids`, `chosen_from_groups`, `external_service_calls`, `output_artifacts`), `artagents/threads/wrapper.py` (`begin(request)` / `finalize(record_id, result)`), modify `artagents/executors/runner.py:75` `run_executor` to call wrapper, modify `artagents/orchestrators/runner.py` symmetrically. Wrapper is no-op when `dry_run=True`, `request.out` under `tempfile.gettempdir()`, `request.thread == "@none"`, or `ARTAGENTS_THREADS_OFF=1` (SD-030). `host_id` per SD-012, `agent_version` from `git rev-parse HEAD` with cached fallback, `inputs_digest` via `xxhash`, `output_artifacts[*].sha256` computed at finalize (SD-032). Acceptance: prefix line printed BEFORE rest of stdout for `executors run` and `orchestrators run`; archive→reopen window honored; reaper marks orphaned PIDs `returncode: -1, status: "orphaned"`. Deliverables: brief snapshotting (`runs/<slug>/brief.copy.txt` + `brief_content_sha256` per SD-009), provenance block in `hype.metadata.json` added by `builtin.cut` per SD-019 (denormalize `thread_label`, `agent_version`), `docs/threads.md` (SD-038), one paragraph appended to `SKILL.md` instructing agents to run `thread show @active` at session start (SD-038), footer line in `executors inspect` / `orchestrators inspect` naming active thread. Backfill: `python3 -m artagents thread backfill` for orphan dirs clustered by hash-graph connected components (SD-034). Acceptance: every SD-008 field is now produced for every run; `hype.metadata.json` carries provenance; `thread_label` survives `rm -rf .artagents/`; full pytest suite passes unmodified. ### Step 6: Author Critical Path + Risks (`docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`)
**Scope:** Small

Note that Day 5 is the latest-possible date for SD-008 to ship; slipping it pollutes the run corpus permanently (irreversible).
2. **Add** `## Risks and Mitigations` table covering at minimum:
   - Lock contention under SD-037 8-parallel test → mitigation: short critical sections (read→mutate→atomic-replace), exponential-backoff retry on `EWOULDBLOCK`, lock-acquisition timing logged in tests.
   - Chokepoint regression breaking existing tests → mitigation: wrapper is no-op when `out` is `tempfile.gettempdir()` or unwritable; full pytest run is gated each day.

2. Resolve open questions (Step 3) before day-by-day plan, since OQs determine concrete day content (parallelism mode, formula, file paths).

6. Closing sections (Step 8) wrap up.

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint Plan Document for Thread/Variant/Iteration-Video Layer

## Overview
Produce `docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`: a 2-week (10 working day) sprint plan that turns `docs/design-thread-layer.md`'s Settled Decisions into day-by-day deliverables. The deliverable is a planning document, not code. Every Settled Decision (SD-001…SD-040) is a hard constraint; SD-035 fixes the week split, SD-036 fixes scope cuts. The document must include: one-page exec summary, day-by-day plan with per-day acceptance criteria, critical path, risks + mitigations, integration test plan (rolling up SD-037), dogfood plan against `runs/artagents_logo_v3`, and explicit resolutions to the six Open Questions in the design doc.

Repo grounding (verified):
- Chokepoint exists: `artagents/executors/runner.py:75` (`run_executor`), and `artagents/orchestrators/runner.py`.
- Variant-producing tools exist: `artagents/executors/generate_image/run.py`, `artagents/orchestrators/logo_ideas/run.py`.
- Fixture exists: `runs/artagents_logo_v3/` with `concepts.json`, `grid.jpg`, `logo-manifest.json`, `logo-plan.json`, `prompts.json`, `images/logo-00N.png`.
- `tests/` is pytest-based with substantial existing coverage that must remain green (SD-034, SD-037).
- `SKILL.md` exists at repo root (touchpoint for SD-038).

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Author the exec summary (`docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`)
Write `# Sprint Plan: Thread / Variant / Iteration-Video Layer` followed by a one-page `## Executive Summary` (~40 lines): goal, why-now (SD-008 irreversibility), scope-in (SD-035), scope-out (SD-036 verbatim), critical path one-liner, top 3 risks with one-line mitigations, definition-of-done.

### Step 2: Author Hard Constraints recap (`docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`)
Add `## Hard Constraints (from design)` mapping each load-bearing SD to the day it lands. Include the fail-closed reminder: default `role: ancillary`; only `generate_image` and `logo_ideas` opt in to `variant` per SD-031.

### Step 3: Author Open Question resolutions (`docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`)
Add `## Open Question Resolutions`. Resolve all 6 OQs:
- OQ-1 day allocation → see Week 1/2 sections below.
- OQ-2 summarize parallelism → `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=4)` (I/O-bound).
- OQ-3 prefix line format → width `min(120, $COLUMNS)`, ANSI color iff TTY and not `$NO_COLOR`, label truncated to 32 chars with `…`, never truncate IDs.
- OQ-4 reaper timing → lazy on first index read per process.
- OQ-5 docs path → `docs/threads.md`.
- OQ-6 quality_floor formula → `0.5*fraction_with_parent_run_ids + 0.3*fraction_with_brief_content_sha256 + 0.2*fraction_with_input_artifact_resolved`, default floor 0.6 per SD-024.

### Step 4: Author Week 1 — Data Layer days 1-5 (`docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`)
Add `## Week 1: Data Layer (Days 1–5)` with `### Day N` subsections (Goal / Deliverables / SDs landed / Acceptance / Tests):
- Day 1: Index foundation — `artagents/threads/{ids,index,schema}.py`, `fcntl.flock` + atomic write + `.bak` rotate (SD-003), Crockford ULIDs (SD-004), schema_version (SD-039), repo-relative paths (SD-040).
- Day 2: `run.json` + chokepoint wrapper — `artagents/threads/{record,wrapper}.py`, modify `artagents/executors/runner.py:75` and `artagents/orchestrators/runner.py`, all SD-008 fields produced, no-op gates per SD-030, `xxhash` for inputs_digest + sha256 for outputs (SD-032), `host_id` (SD-012), `agent_version`.
- Day 3: Auto-attribute + prefix lines + lifecycle CLI — `artagents/threads/{attribute,prefix}.py`, 5-branch decision (SD-006) including lineage inference, prefix per SD-018, `thread {new,list,show,archive,reopen}` (SD-007), reaper (SD-033) lazy.
- Day 4: Variants primitive + selection — extend `output_artifact` (SD-013, SD-026), `selections.jsonl` (SD-014), `groups.json` (SD-016), `chosen_from_groups` edge (SD-015), 5 gestures only per SD-017, `[variants]` nag, executor patches per SD-031.
- Day 5: Brief snapshot + provenance + Week-1 polish — `brief.copy.txt` + `brief_content_sha256` (SD-009), provenance block in `hype.metadata.json` (SD-019), `docs/threads.md` + SKILL.md paragraph + inspect footer (SD-038), `thread backfill` (SD-034).

### Step 5: Author Week 2 — Iteration Video MVP days 6-10 (`docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`)
Add `## Week 2: Iteration Video MVP (Days 6–10)`:
- Day 6: Modality registry + `iteration.collect` — `artagents/modalities/{image_grid,audio_waveform,generic_card}.py` (SD-025), `python3 -m artagents modalities {list,inspect}`, `artagents/executors/iteration_collect/` provenance-graph walk (SD-023), data_quality compute (OQ-6).
- Day 7: `iteration.summarize` + `iteration.score` — parallel `builtin.understand` via ThreadPoolExecutor max_workers=4 (OQ-2), brief-diff captions, deterministic ordering.
- Day 8: `iteration.assemble` + quality floor — `(kind,preview_modes)` → renderer (SD-026), precedence `theme>direction>style>defaults` (SD-027), audio-bed selection (SD-028, never generative), quality floor refusal with backfill commands (SD-024), `--force` logged in provenance.
- Day 9: `builtin.iteration_video` orchestrator + report + loud fallback — chain SD-021, 5-output variant group SD-022, `inspect` subcommand + 5 flags (SD-027), `generic_card` loud-fallback annotation + stdout warning (SD-029).
- Day 10: Dogfood + integration suite + buffer — render `runs/artagents_logo_v3`, all 5 outputs, SD-037 suite green, full pytest green.

### Step 6: Author Critical Path + Risks (`docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`)
Add `## Critical Path` (Day-1 atomic write → Day-2 wrapper → Day-3 auto-attribute → Day-4 variants → Day-5 brief+provenance → Day-6 collect → Day-8 assemble → Day-9 orchestrator → Day-10 dogfood; Day 5 is the latest possible date for SD-008 metadata). Add `## Risks and Mitigations` covering: lock contention, chokepoint regression, fixture insufficiency, schema bug post-Day-2 (SD-039 migration path), `builtin.understand` rate limits, scope-creep into SD-036.

### Step 7: Author Integration Test Plan + Dogfood Plan (`docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`)
Add `## Integration Test Plan` mapping SD-037 line items to concrete test file paths (`tests/test_threads_index.py`, `tests/test_threads_attribute.py`, `tests/test_threads_concurrent.py`, `tests/test_threads_variants.py`, `tests/test_threads_provenance.py`, `tests/test_iteration_video.py`) plus a daily-regression gate (full pytest each day). Add `## Dogfood Plan: runs/artagents_logo_v3` with day-tagged steps: Day 5 backfill (verify roles on logo-00N.png variants vs grid.jpg ancillary), Day 9 inspect, Day 10 render with concrete expected outputs and a manual checklist.

### Step 8: Author closing sections (`docs/sprint-thread-layer.md`)
Add `## Out of Scope` (SD-036 verbatim), `## Documentation Deliverables` (mapping SD-038 to docs/threads.md, SKILL.md paragraph, inspect footer — all Day 5), and `## Definition of Done` (SD-008 fields produced for every run after Day 2; SD-037 green; full pytest green; dogfood produces all 5 SD-022 outputs; docs updated; zero modifications to executor `run.py` outside SD-031).

## Execution Order
1. Steps 1-2 (exec summary + constraints) first.
2. Step 3 (OQ resolutions) before day-by-day, since OQs feed concrete day content.
3. Step 4 (Week 1) before Step 5 (Week 2) — Week 2 depends on Week 1.
4. Step 6 (critical path + risks) after both weeks scoped.
5. Step 7 (integration + dogfood) — aggregates concrete file paths from Steps 4-5.
6. Step 8 (closing) last.

## Validation Order
1. Confirm every load-bearing SD appears in some day's "SDs landed" line or in scope/cuts.
2. Confirm all 6 OQs are explicitly resolved.
3. Confirm exec summary fits ~one page.
4. Confirm dogfood names exact files in `runs/artagents_logo_v3/`.
5. Confirm only `docs/sprint-thread-layer.md` is authored.

        Requirements:
        - Before addressing individual flags, check: does any flag suggest the plan is targeting the wrong code or the wrong root cause? If so, consider whether the plan needs a new approach rather than adjustments. Explain your reasoning.
        - Update the plan to address the significant issues.
        - Keep the plan readable and executable.
        - Return flags_addressed with the exact flag IDs you addressed.
        - Include `changes_summary` as a short plain-English summary of what changed in the revision. If there were no concrete flags, say that explicitly (for example: `No critique flags were raised; refined wording and kept the plan aligned for execution.`).
        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`. Promote or demote priorities if critique feedback reveals a criterion was over- or under-weighted.
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Verify that the plan remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal plan quality.
        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.
        - Maintain the structural template: H1 title, ## Overview, phase sections with numbered step sections, ## Execution Order or ## Validation Order.
        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised plan markdown (all sections) must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.
        - CRITICAL: Return only the structured JSON object for the schema fields `plan`, `changes_summary`, `flags_addressed`, `assumptions`, `success_criteria`, and `questions`. Do not add commentary before or after the JSON object.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

tool_result Read
```
189	- **SD-040** — All paths in persisted JSON are repo-relative or content-addressed. No absolute paths. This makes thread export/sharing (a future feature) work for free as long as we hold the line now. _load_bearing: true_
190	
191	- **SD-041** — Privacy / redaction policy. Briefs and prompts may contain PII or NDA-bound content. Today everything is plaintext under `runs/` and gets stamped into `run.json` and `external_service_calls`. The sprint must (a) document that `runs/` is gitignored and treated as local-only; (b) define a `secret`-prefixed env var convention that strips matching values from `cli_args_redacted`; (c) add a `--no-content` flag to `thread show` and the iteration-video HTML report that displays only hashes and labels for sensitive content. **Briefs themselves are not redacted by default** — the user opts in by placing them under `runs/<slug>/private/` (a documented convention). _load_bearing: true_
192	  Rationale: a thread is a creative artifact a user may want to share; the moment sharing exists, redaction must already work or the data is poisoned.
193	
194	- **SD-042** — Concurrent variant-selection semantics. Two agents (or two terminals) may `thread keep` the same group with conflicting choices simultaneously. The append-only `selections.jsonl` makes this safe but the rule must be **explicitly documented for agents in `SKILL.md` and in the `[variants]` line help text**: "selections are append-only; the most recent write is authoritative on read; prior selections are preserved as history but do not affect current keepers." No locking on selection writes (the file is per-thread + append-only + line-buffered, so atomic appends are safe). _load_bearing: true_
195	
196	- **SD-043** — Iteration-video cost guardrail. A 47-iteration thread generates ~47 `builtin.understand` calls in `iteration.summarize`. At realistic per-call costs ($0.005–$0.10) this is $0.25–$5 per render, which is fine — but a thread with 500 iterations or repeated re-renders is not. The sprint must (a) cap `iteration.summarize` at `max_iterations` (default 200, configurable, with a clear refusal message above the cap); (b) cache summaries by `(run_id, summarizer_model_version)` keyed in `.artagents/iteration_cache/` so re-renders only summarize new iterations; (c) surface estimated cost in `iteration_video inspect` output **before** the agent commits to a render. _load_bearing: true_
197	  Rationale: silent cost blowups erode trust; the agent must see the bill before incurring it.
198	
199	- **SD-044** — Recorded tradeoffs (for future readers, not load-bearing). Two design choices were debated and settled but the alternatives are worth preserving so future maintainers don't relitigate:
200	  - **Scalar `thread_id` vs pure DAG-derived membership.** Settled as scalar (per SD-002 / SD-008) because (i) root runs with no consumed artifacts have no causal edge and need *some* attribution mechanism; (ii) membership lookups are O(1) field reads instead of graph traversals, which matters for the per-run `[thread]` prefix line; (iii) the agent's mental model needs a name to grab. The DAG (via `parent_run_ids` + `chosen_from_groups`) is preserved alongside the scalar and powers `thread doctor` heuristics, but membership is the scalar. If true multi-thread membership is ever needed (a single run that's a first-class member of two threads), this will need to flip.
201	  - **`thread` vs other nouns** (`pursuit`, `take`, `session`, `workstream`). Settled as `thread` because creators already say "pull on this thread" and the term appears in every prefix line where shortness matters. Git overload exists but is tolerable in the creative-tools domain. If git terminology bleeds in heavily (e.g., agents start typing `git thread` by mistake), revisit.
202	  _load_bearing: false_
203	
204	## Open questions (for the sprint plan to resolve)
205	
206	These are not settled; the sprint plan should make a call:
207	
208	- Day-by-day allocation within each week (the broad week split is settled; the per-day breakdown is not).
209	- Whether `iteration.summarize` parallelism uses Python threads, asyncio, or sequential calls in the v1 MVP.
210	- Exact format of the prefix line beyond what SD-018 specifies (terminal width handling, color, truncation rules).
211	- Whether the in-flight reaper (SD-033) runs at process start or lazily on first index read.
212	- Where the `WORKSTREAMS.md` / `THREADS.md` global doc lives (likely `docs/threads.md`, but confirm against repo conventions).
213	- Concrete `quality_floor` formula in `iteration.collect`: which signals contribute and with what weights.
214	
215	## Non-goals
216	
217	- Cross-machine sync of threads. (Capture `host_id` per SD-012 to enable later.)
218	- Public API stability of any of the new schemas across major versions.
219	- Migration tooling for runs produced before the layer ships. (Backfill per SD-034 is best-effort.)
220	- A general-purpose web UI for browsing threads. (`iteration.report.html` per orchestrator output is the only HTML deliverable.)
221
```
